## Economy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Economy")]

Main article: [Economy of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Albania "Economy of Albania")

|                                                                                                                                                   |                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png) | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(May 2023)* |

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Toptani_Shopping_Mall_Tirana_2016.jpg/220px-Toptani_Shopping_Mall_Tirana_2016.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Toptani_Shopping_Mall_Tirana_2016.jpg)

[Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") is the economic hub of the country. It is home to major domestic and foreign companies operating in the country.

Albania's transition from a socialist [planned economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy "Planned economy") to a capitalist [mixed economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy "Mixed economy") has been largely successful.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-worlddiplomacy-212) The country has a [developing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developing_country "Developing country") mixed economy classified by the [World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank "World Bank") as an [upper-middle income economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_\(nominal,_Atlas_method\)_per_capita#Upper-middle-income_group "List of countries by GNI (nominal, Atlas method) per capita"). In 2016, it had the fourth-lowest [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_unemployment_rate "List of countries by unemployment rate") in the [Balkans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkans "Balkans") with an estimated value of 14.7%. Its largest trading partners are Italy, Greece, China, Spain, Kosovo and the United States. The [lek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_lek "Albanian lek") (ALL) is the country's currency and is [pegged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fixed_exchange_rate "Fixed exchange rate") at approximately 132.51 lek per euro.

The cities of [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs "Durrës") constitute the economic and financial heart of Albania due to their high population, modern infrastructure and strategic geographical location. The country's most important infrastructure facilities take course through both of the cities, connecting the north to the south as well as the west to the east. Among the largest [companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_of_Albania "List of companies of Albania") are the energy distribution public company [OSHEE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operatori_i_Shp%C3%ABrndarjes_s%C3%AB_Energjis%C3%AB_Elektrike "Operatori i Shpërndarjes së Energjisë Elektrike"), steel producer Kurum, oil cdompanies like Kastrati, [Albpetrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albpetrol "Albpetrol"), and [ARMO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARMO_oil_refiner "ARMO oil refiner"), the mineral [AlbChrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AlbChrome "AlbChrome"), the investment [BALFIN Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samir_Mane "Samir Mane") and the telecommunications companies [One Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albtelecom "Albtelecom") and [Vodafone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Albania "Vodafone Albania").[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-213)

In 2012, Albania's [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_per_capita "GDP per capita") stood at 30% of the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union "European Union") average, while [GDP (PPP) per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita "GDP (PPP) per capita") was 35%.[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-214) In the first quarter of 2010, after the [Great Recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession "Great Recession"), Albania was one of three countries in Europe to record economic growth.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-215)[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-216) The [International Monetary Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund "International Monetary Fund") predicted 2.6% growth for Albania in 2010 and 3.2% in 2011.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-217) According to *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")*, as of December 2016[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit), the [Gross Domestic Product (GDP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_Domestic_Product_\(GDP\) "Gross Domestic Product (GDP)") was growing at 2.8%. The country had a [trade balance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_balance "Trade balance") of −9.7% and [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_rate "Unemployment rate") of 14.7%.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-218) [Foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment "Foreign direct investment") has increased significantly in recent years as the government has embarked on an ambitious programme to improve the business climate through fiscal and legislative reforms.

### Primary sector

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Primary sector")]

Main article: [Agriculture in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Albania "Agriculture in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Berat_kale_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Berat_kale_-_panoramio.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Berat_kale_-_panoramio.jpg)

Grapes in [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat "Berat"). Due to the [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate "Mediterranean climate"), [wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_wine "Albanian wine"), [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive "Olive") and [citrus fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citrus_fruit "Citrus fruit") are mostly produced in Southern Albania.

Agriculture in the country is based on small to medium-sized family-owned dispersed units. It remains a significant sector of the [economy of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Albania "Economy of Albania"). It employs 41%[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-219) of the population, and about 24.31% of the land is used for agricultural purposes. One of the earliest farming sites in Europe has been found in the southeast of the country.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-220) As part of the pre-accession process of Albania to the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union "European Union"), farmers are being aided through [IPA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instrument_for_Pre-Accession_Assistance "Instrument for Pre-Accession Assistance") funds to improve Albanian agriculture standards.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-221)

Albania produces significant amounts of fruits (apples, [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive "Olive"), grapes, oranges, lemons, [apricots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apricot "Apricot"), [peaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach "Peach"), [cherries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry "Cherry"), [figs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figs "Figs"), [sour cherries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prunus_cerasus "Prunus cerasus"), [plums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plum "Plum"), and [strawberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strawberry "Strawberry")), [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetable_oil "Vegetable oil") (potatoes, tomatoes, [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize "Maize"), onions, and wheat), [sugar beets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_beet "Sugar beet"), tobacco, meat, [honey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honey "Honey"), [dairy products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_product "Dairy product"), traditional medicine and [aromatic plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essential_oil "Essential oil"). Further, the country is a worldwide significant producer of [salvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvia "Salvia"), [rosemary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemary "Rosemary") and [yellow gentian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentiana_lutea "Gentiana lutea").[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-222) The country's proximity to the [Ionian Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionian_Sea "Ionian Sea") and the [Adriatic Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic_Sea "Adriatic Sea") give the underdeveloped fishing industry great potential. The [World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank "World Bank") and [European Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Community "European Community") economists report that, Albania's fishing industry has good potential to generate export earnings because prices in the nearby Greek and Italian markets are many times higher than those in the Albanian market. The fish available off the coasts of the country are [carp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carp "Carp"), [trout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trout "Trout"), [sea bream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_bream "Sea bream"), [mussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mussel "Mussel") and [crustaceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean "Crustacean").

Albania has one of Europe's longest histories of [viticulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viticulture "Viticulture").[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-winealbania1-223) Today's region was one of the few places where vine was naturally grown during the ice age. The oldest found seeds in the region are 4,000 to 6,000 years old.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Stevenson2011-224) In 2009, the nation produced an estimated 17,500 tonnes of wine.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-225)

### Secondary sector

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Secondary sector")]

See also: [Category:Mines in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category:Mines_in_Albania "Category:Mines in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Cement_factory_in_Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB%2C_Albania_%2810759257413%29.jpg/220px-Cement_factory_in_Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB%2C_Albania_%2810759257413%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cement_factory_in_Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB,_Albania_\(10759257413\).jpg)

The [Antea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antea_Cement "Antea Cement") factory in [Fushë-Krujë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB "Fushë-Krujë")

Albania's secondary sector has undergone many changes and diversification since the communist regime collapsed. It is very diversified, from [electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics_industry "Electronics industry"), [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_industries "Manufacturing industries"),[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-226) [textiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textile_industry "Textile industry"), to [food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_industry "Food industry"), [cement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cement_industry "Cement industry"), [mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry "Mining industry"),[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-227) and [energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_industry "Energy industry"). The [Antea Cement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antea_Cement "Antea Cement") plant in [Fushë-Krujë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB "Fushë-Krujë") is considered one of the nation's largest industrial greenfield investments.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-228) Albanian oil and gas is one of the most promising, albeit strictly regulated, sectors of its economy. Albania has the [second-largest oil deposits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves "List of countries by proven oil reserves") in the [Balkan peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkan_peninsula "Balkan peninsula") after [Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania "Romania"), and the largest [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patos-Marinza_Oil_Field "Patos-Marinza Oil Field")[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-229) in Europe. The [Albpetrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albpetrol "Albpetrol") company is owned by the Albanian state and monitors the state petroleum agreements in the country. The textile industry has seen an extensive expansion by approaching companies from the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union "European Union") (EU) in Albania. According to the [Instituti i Statistikës (INSTAT)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Statistics_\(Albania\) "Institute of Statistics (Albania)"), as of 2016[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit), textile production had an annual growth of 5.3% and an annual turnover of around 1.5 billion euros.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-230)

Albania is a significant minerals producer and ranks among the world's leading [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium "Chromium") producers and exporters.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-231) The nation is also a notable producer of copper, nickel, and coal.[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-232) The [Batra mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batra_mine "Batra mine"), [Bulqizë mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulqiz%C3%AB_mine "Bulqizë mine"), and [Thekna mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thekna_mine "Thekna mine") are among the most recognised Albanian mines still in operation.

### Tertiary sector

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=27 "Edit section: Tertiary sector")]

See also: [Banking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_Albania "Banking in Albania"), [Telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Albania "Telecommunications in Albania"), and [Tourism in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Albania "Tourism in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Ksamil_Albania_._Albanian_Riviera.jpg/220px-Ksamil_Albania_._Albanian_Riviera.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ksamil_Albania_._Albanian_Riviera.jpg)

The [Islets of Ksamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islets_of_Ksamil "Islets of Ksamil"), in the south of the [Albanian Ionian Sea Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Ionian_Sea_Coast "Albanian Ionian Sea Coast")

The [tertiary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector "Tertiary sector") represents the fastest growing sector of the country's economy. 36% of the population work in the service sector which contributes to 65% of the country's GDP.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DSSA-233) Ever since the end of the 20th century, the [banking industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_industry "Banking industry") is a major component of the tertiary sector and remains in good conditions overall due to [privatisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatisation "Privatisation") and the commendable [monetary policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monetary_policy "Monetary policy").[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-234)[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DSSA-233)

Previously one of the most [isolated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolationism "Isolationism") and controlled countries in the world, [telecommunication industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication_industry "Telecommunication industry") represents nowadays another major contributor to the sector. It developed largely through privatisation and subsequent investment by both domestic and foreign investors.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DSSA-233) [Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Mobile "Eagle Mobile"), [Vodafone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Albania "Vodafone Albania") and [Telekom Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Albania "Telekom Albania") are the leading [telecommunications service providers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_service_provider "Telecommunications service provider") in the country.

Tourism is recognised as an industry of national importance and has been steadily increasing since the beginnings of the 21st century.[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-235)[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-236) It directly accounted for 8.4% of GDP in 2016 though including indirect contributions pushes the proportion to 26%.[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-237) In the same year, the country received approximately 4.74 million visitors mostly from across Europe and the United States as well.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-238)

The increase of foreign visitors has been dramatic. Albania had only 500,000 visitors in 2005, and an estimated 4.2 million in 2012, an increase of 740 percent. In 2015, summer tourism increased by 25 percent from 2014, according to the country's tourism agency.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-239) In 2011, [Lonely Planet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonely_Planet "Lonely Planet") named Albania as a top travel destination,[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Lonely_Planet's_top_10-240)\[*[failed verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")*] while *[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")* placed Albania as number 4 global tourist destination in 2014.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-241)

The bulk of the tourist industry is concentrated along the [Adriatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic "Adriatic") and [Ionian Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionian_Sea "Ionian Sea") in the west of the country. But the [Albanian Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Riviera "Albanian Riviera") in the southwest has the most scenic and pristine beaches; its coastline has a considerable length of 446 kilometres (277 miles).[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-242) The coast has a distinctive character, rich in varieties of virgin beaches, capes, coves, covered bays, lagoons, small gravel beaches, sea caves, and many landforms. Some parts of this seaside are very clean ecologically, including unexplored areas, which are very rare within the [Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean "Mediterranean").[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-243) Other attractions include the mountainous areas such as the [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Alps "Albanian Alps"), [Ceraunian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceraunian_Mountains "Ceraunian Mountains") and [Korab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korab "Korab") Mountains but also the historical cities of [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat "Berat"), [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs "Durrës"), [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr "Gjirokastër"), [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB "Sarandë"), [Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shkod%C3%ABr "Shkodër") and [Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB "Korçë").

### Transport

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Transport")]

Main article: [Transport in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Albania "Transport in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Autostrada_Durr%C3%ABs-Morina-05.jpg/220px-Autostrada_Durr%C3%ABs-Morina-05.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Autostrada_Durr%C3%ABs-Morina-05.jpg)

[Rruga e Kombit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs-Kuk%C3%ABs_Highway "Durrës-Kukës Highway") connects the [Adriatic Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic_Sea "Adriatic Sea") across the [Western Lowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Lowlands "Western Lowlands") with the [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Alps "Albanian Alps").

Transportation in Albania is managed within the functions of the [Ministry of Infrastructure and Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Infrastructure_and_Energy_\(Albania\) "Ministry of Infrastructure and Energy (Albania)") and entities such as the [Albanian Road Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Road_Authority "Albanian Road Authority") (ARRSH), responsible for the construction and maintenance of the [highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highways_in_Albania "Highways in Albania") and [motorways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorways_in_Albania "Motorways in Albania") in Albania, as well as the [Albanian Aviation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Aviation_Authority_\(Albania\) "Civil Aviation Authority (Albania)") (AAC), with the responsibility of coordinating civil aviation and airports in the country.

The [international airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_International_Airport_N%C3%ABn%C3%AB_Tereza "Tirana International Airport Nënë Tereza") of [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") is the premier air gateway to the country, and is also the principal hub for Albania's national [flag carrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_carrier "Flag carrier") airline, [Air Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Albania "Air Albania"). The airport carried more than 3.3 million passengers in 2019 with connections to many destinations in other countries around [Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe "Europe"), [Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa "Africa") and [Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia "Asia").[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-244) The country plans to progressively increase the number of airports especially in the south with possible locations in [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB "Sarandë"), [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr "Gjirokastër") and [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB_International_Airport "Vlorë International Airport").[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-245)

The [highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highways_in_Albania "Highways in Albania") and [motorways in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorways_in_Albania "Motorways in Albania") are properly maintained and often still under construction and renovation. The [Autostrada 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A1_motorway_\(Albania\) "A1 motorway (Albania)") (A1) is an integral transportation corridor and the country's longest motorway. It is planned to link [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs "Durrës") on the Adriatic Sea across [Pristina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pristina "Pristina") in Kosovo with the [Pan-European Corridor X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_X "Pan-European Corridor X") in Serbia.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SEETO-246)[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-247) The [Autostrada 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A2_motorway_\(Albania\) "A2 motorway (Albania)") (A2) is part of the [Adriatic–Ionian Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic%E2%80%93Ionian_motorway "Adriatic–Ionian motorway") as well as the [Pan-European Corridor VIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_VIII "Pan-European Corridor VIII") and connects [Fier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fier "Fier") with [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB "Vlorë").[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SEETO-246) The [Autostrada 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A3_motorway_\(Albania\) "A3 motorway (Albania)") (A3) is under construction and after its completion will connect [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") and [Elbasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbasan "Elbasan") with the Pan-European Corridor VIII. When all three corridors are completed, Albania will have an estimated 759 kilometres (472 mi) of highway, linking it with all neighboring countries.

[Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Durr%C3%ABs "Port of Durrës") is the busiest and largest [seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seaport "Seaport") in the country, followed by [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Vlor%C3%AB "Port of Vlorë"), [Shëngjin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Sh%C3%ABngjin "Port of Shëngjin") and [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Sarand%C3%AB "Port of Sarandë"). As of 2014[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit), it is as one of the largest passenger ports on the [Adriatic Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic_Sea "Adriatic Sea"), with annual passenger volume of about 1.5 million. The principal ports serve a system of ferries connecting Albania with islands and coastal cities in Croatia, Greece, and Italy.

The rail network is administered by the national railway company [Hekurudha Shqiptare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hekurudha_Shqiptare "Hekurudha Shqiptare"), which was extensively promoted by Hoxha. There has been considerable increase in private car ownership and bus usage while rail use decreased since the end of communism. A new railway line from Tirana and its airport to Durrës is planned. The location of this railway, connecting Albania's most populated urban areas, makes it an important economic development project.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-248)[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-249)

## Infrastructure

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Infrastructure")]

### Education

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Education")]

Main article: [Education in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Albania "Education in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Akademia_e_arteve_Ergys_Veliu_Photography.jpg/220px-Akademia_e_arteve_Ergys_Veliu_Photography.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Akademia_e_arteve_Ergys_Veliu_Photography.jpg)

The [University of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Arts_\(Albania\) "University of Arts (Albania)") is the largest higher education institute dedicated to the study of arts

In Albania, education is secular, free, [compulsory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education "Compulsory education"), and based on three levels.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Official-250)[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Epnuffic-251) The academic year is apportioned into two semesters, beginning in September or October and ending in June or July. [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") is the [primary language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_language "Primary language") of instruction in the country's [academic institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_school "State school").[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Epnuffic-251) The study of a first [foreign language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_language "Foreign language") is mandatory and taught most often at elementary and bilingual schools.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-foreign_languages-252) Languages taught in schools are English, Italian, French and German.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-foreign_languages-252) Albania has a [school life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_life_expectancy "School life expectancy") of 16 years and a [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate "Literacy rate") of 98.7%, with 99.2% for men and 98.3% for women.[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-253)[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia_fb2013-254)

Compulsory primary education is divided into two levels, elementary and secondary school, from grade one to five and six to nine, respectively.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Official-250) Pupils are required to attend school from the age six until they turn 16. Upon successful completion of primary education, all pupils are entitled to attend high schools, specialising in any field, including arts, sports, languages, sciences, and technology.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Official-250)

Tertiary education is optional and has undergone a thorough reformation and restructuring in compliance with the principles of the [Bologna Process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna_Process "Bologna Process"). There are a significant number of private and public [institutions of higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutions_of_higher_education "Institutions of higher education") in Albania's major cities.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-255)[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Epnuffic-251) Tertiary education is organised into three successive levels, the [bachelor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor%27s_degree "Bachelor's degree"), [master](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master%27s_degree "Master's degree"), and [doctorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctorate_Degree "Doctorate Degree").

### Health

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Health")]

Main article: [Health in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Albania "Health in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Olive-oil-1412361_1920.jpg/220px-Olive-oil-1412361_1920.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Olive-oil-1412361_1920.jpg)

The [Albanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_cuisine "Albanian cuisine") from the Mediterranean, which is characterised by the use of fruits, vegetables and [olive oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive_oil "Olive oil"), contributes to the good nutrition of the country's population[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-256)

The [constitution of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Albania "Constitution of Albania") guarantees its citizens equal, free, and [universal health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care "Universal health care").[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-257) The health care system is organised into [primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_healthcare "Primary healthcare"), [secondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_care "Secondary care"), and [tertiary healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_healthcare "Tertiary healthcare"), and is in a process of modernisation and development.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cod-258)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-hospitals-259) The [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy "Life expectancy") at birth in Albania is 77.8 years, ranking [37th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy "List of countries by life expectancy") in the world and surpassing several [developed countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_countries "Developed countries").[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-260) The average [healthy life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy#Healthy_life_expectancy "Life expectancy") is 68.8 years, ranking 37th in the world.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-261) The country's [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate "Infant mortality rate") was estimated at 12 per 1,000 live births in 2015. In 2000, the country had the world's 55th-best healthcare performance, as defined by the [World Health Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Health_Organization "World Health Organization") (WHO).[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-262)

[Cardiovascular disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiovascular_disease "Cardiovascular disease") is the principal cause of death in Albania, accounting for 52% of deaths.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cod-258) [Accidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accident "Accident"), injuries, [malignant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malignant_disease "Malignant disease") and [respiratory diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_disease "Respiratory disease") are other primary causes of death.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cod-258) [Neuropsychiatric disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuropsychiatric_disorders "Neuropsychiatric disorders") has also increased due to recent demographic, social, and economic changes in the country.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cod-258)

In 2009, Albania had a fruit and vegetable supply of 886 grams per capita per day, the fifth-highest supply in Europe.[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-263) Compared to other developed and developing countries, Albania has a relatively low rate of [obesity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obesity "Obesity"), probably thanks to the [Mediterranean diet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_diet "Mediterranean diet").[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-264)[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-265) According to [2016 WHO data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Body_Mass_Index_\(BMI\)#WHO_Data_on_Prevalence_of_Obesity_\(BMI_above_30\)_\(2014\) "List of countries by Body Mass Index (BMI)"), 21.7% of adults in the country are clinically [overweight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overweight "Overweight"), with a [Body mass index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_mass_index "Body mass index") (BMI) score of 25 or more.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-266)

### Energy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: Energy")]

Main article: [Renewable energy in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Albania "Renewable energy in Albania")

See also: [Power stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Albania "List of power stations in Albania") and [Oil fields in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oil_and_gas_fields_in_Albania "List of oil and gas fields in Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Albania_electricity_production.svg/220px-Albania_electricity_production.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Albania_electricity_production.svg)

Electricity production in Albania from 1980 to 2019

Due to its location and natural resources, Albania has a wide variety of [energy resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_resource "Energy resource"), ranging from gas, oil, and coal to [wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_energy "Wind energy"), [solar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_energy "Solar energy"), [water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydropower "Hydropower"), and other [renewable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy "Renewable energy") sources.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-267)[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-268) According to the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Economic_Forum "World Economic Forum")'s 2023 Energy Transition Index (ETI), the country ranked 21st globally, highlighting the progress in its energy transition agenda.[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-269) Currently, Albania's electricity generation sector depends on [hydroelectricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectricity "Hydroelectricity"), ranking fifth in the world in percentage terms.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-270)[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-271)[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-272) Increasing risks of river floods and droughts due to [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") is expected to put electricity generation at risk.[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-273) The [Drin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drin_\(river\) "Drin (river)"), in the north, hosts four [hydroelectric power stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectric_power_station "Hydroelectric power station"), including [Fierza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fierza_Hydroelectric_Power_Station "Fierza Hydroelectric Power Station"), [Koman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koman_Hydroelectric_Power_Station "Koman Hydroelectric Power Station"), [Skavica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skavica_Hydro_Power_Plant "Skavica Hydro Power Plant") and [Vau i Dejës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vau_i_Dej%C3%ABs_Hydroelectric_Power_Station "Vau i Dejës Hydroelectric Power Station"). Two other power stations, such as the [Banjë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banj%C3%AB_Hydro_Power_Plant "Banjë Hydro Power Plant") and [Moglicë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moglic%C3%AB_Hydro_Power_Plant "Moglicë Hydro Power Plant"), are along the [Devoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devoll_\(river\) "Devoll (river)") in the south.[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-274)

Albania has considerable oil deposits. It has the 10th-largest oil reserves in Europe and the 58th in the world.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-275) The country's main petroleum deposits are located around the [Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Adriatic_Sea_Coast "Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast") and [Myzeqe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myzeqe "Myzeqe") Plain within the [Western Lowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Lowlands "Western Lowlands"), where the country's largest reserve is located. [Patos-Marinza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patos-Marinza_Oil_Field "Patos-Marinza Oil Field"), also located within the area, is the largest onshore [oil field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_field "Oil field") in Europe.[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-276) The [Trans Adriatic Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Adriatic_Pipeline "Trans Adriatic Pipeline") (TAP), part of the planned [Southern Gas Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Gas_Corridor "Southern Gas Corridor"), runs for 215 kilometres (134 miles) across Albania's territory before entering the [Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Adriatic_Sea_Coast "Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast") approximately 17 kilometres (11 miles) northwest of [Fier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fier "Fier").[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-277)

Albania's [water resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_resources "Water resources") are particularly abundant in all the regions of the country and comprise [lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakes_of_Albania "Lakes of Albania"), [rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivers_of_Albania "Rivers of Albania"), springs, and groundwater aquifers.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-278) The country's available average quantity of [fresh water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresh_water "Fresh water") is estimated at 129.7 cubic metres (4,580 cubic feet) per inhabitant per year, one of the highest rates in Europe.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-279) According to data presented by the [Joint Monitoring Programme for Water Supply and Sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Monitoring_Programme_for_Water_Supply_and_Sanitation "Joint Monitoring Programme for Water Supply and Sanitation") (JMP) in 2015, about 93% of the country's total population had access to [improved sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation "Improved sanitation").[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-280)

### Media

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Media")]

Main article: [Media in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Albania "Media in Albania")

See also: [Cinema of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Albania "Cinema of Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Villa_of_the_former_Radio_Tirana_%2803%29.jpg/220px-Villa_of_the_former_Radio_Tirana_%2803%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Villa_of_the_former_Radio_Tirana_\(03\).jpg)

The former grounds of the headquarters of [Radio Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Tirana "Radio Tirana") in the capital of [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana"). [Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar "Radio Televizioni Shqiptar") (RTSH) was initially inaugurated as Radio Tirana in 1938 prior to the [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II")

The [freedom of press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_press "Freedom of press") and [speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech "Freedom of speech"), and the right to free expression is guaranteed in the [constitution of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Albania "Constitution of Albania").[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-281) Albania was ranked 84th on the [Press Freedom Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index "Press Freedom Index") of 2020 compiled by the [Reporters Without Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders "Reporters Without Borders"), with its score steadily declining since 2003.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-282) Nevertheless, in the 2020 report of [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World "Freedom in the World"), the [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House "Freedom House") classified the freedoms of press and speech in Albania as partly free from political interference and manipulation.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-283)

[Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar "Radio Televizioni Shqiptar") (RTSH) is the [national broadcaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_broadcaster "National broadcaster") corporation of Albania operating numerous television and radio stations in the country.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-284) The three major private broadcaster corporations are [Top Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_Channel "Top Channel"), [Televizioni Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Klan "TV Klan") and [Vizion Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizion_Plus "Vizion Plus") whose content are distributed throughout Albania and beyond its territory in [Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo "Kosovo") and other [Albanian-speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") territories.

Albanian cinema has its roots in the 20th century and developed after the country's [declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Declaration_of_Independence "Albanian Declaration of Independence").[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Cinema-285) The first [movie theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_theater "Movie theater") exclusively devoted to showing [motion pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_pictures "Motion pictures") was built in 1912 in [Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shkod%C3%ABr "Shkodër").[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Cinema-285) During the [People's Republic of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_Albania "People's Republic of Albania"), Albanian cinema developed rapidly with the inauguration of the [Kinostudio Shqipëria e Re](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinostudio_Shqip%C3%ABria_e_Re "Kinostudio Shqipëria e Re") in Tirana.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Cinema-285) In 1953, the Albanian-Soviet [epic film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_film "Epic film"), the [Great Warrior Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Great_Warrior_Skanderbeg "The Great Warrior Skanderbeg"), was released chronicling the life and fight of the medieval Albanian hero [Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjergj_Kastriot_Skanderbeg "Gjergj Kastriot Skanderbeg"). It went on to win the international prize at the [1954 Cannes Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Cannes_Film_Festival "1954 Cannes Film Festival"). In 2003, the [Tirana International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_International_Film_Festival "Tirana International Film Festival") was established, the largest film festival in the country. The [Durrës Amphitheatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_Amphitheatre "Durrës Amphitheatre") is host to the [Durrës International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_International_Film_Festival "Durrës International Film Festival"), the second largest film festival.

### Technology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Technology")]

Main articles: [Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Albania "Science and technology in Albania") and [Telecommunications in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Albania "Telecommunications in Albania")

After the [fall of communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_communism_in_Albania "Fall of communism in Albania") in 1991, human resources in sciences and technology in Albania have drastically decreased. As of various reports, during 1991 to 2005 approximately 50% of the professors and scientists of the universities and science institutions in the country have left Albania.[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-dfid-286) In 2009, the [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Albania "Government of Albania") approved the National Strategy for Science, Technology and Innovation in Albania covering the period 2009 to 2015.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-287) It aims to triple public spending on [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development "Research and development") to 0.6% of GDP and augment the share of [GDE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_domestic_expenditure_on_R%26D "Gross domestic expenditure on R\&D") from foreign sources, including the framework programmes for research of the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union "European Union"), to the point where it covers 40% of research spending, among others. Albania was ranked 84th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2024.[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-288)

Telecommunication represents one of the fastest growing and dynamic sectors in Albania.[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-National_Plan-289)[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-290) [Vodafone Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Albania "Vodafone Albania"), [Telekom Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Albania "Telekom Albania") and [Albtelecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albtelecom "Albtelecom") are the three large providers of [mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_network_operator "Mobile network operator") and [internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet "Internet") in Albania.[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-National_Plan-289) As of the [Electronic and Postal Communications Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_and_Postal_Communications_Authority_\(Albania\) "Electronic and Postal Communications Authority (Albania)") (AKEP) in 2018, the country had approximately 2.7 million active mobile users with almost 1.8 million active broadband subscribers.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AKEP-291) Vodafone Albania alone served more than 931,000 mobile users, Telekom Albania had about 605,000 users and Albtelecom had more than 272,000 users.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AKEP-291) In [January 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_spaceflight_launches_in_January%E2%80%93June_2023 "List of spaceflight launches in January–June 2023"), Albania launched its [first two satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_first_satellites_by_country "List of first satellites by country"), *Albania 1* and *Albania 2*, into [orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orbit "Orbit"), in what was regarded as a milestone effort in monitoring the country's territory and identifying illegal activities.[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-292)[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-293) Albanian-American engineer [Mira Murati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mira_Murati "Mira Murati"), the Chief Technology Officer of research organisation [OpenAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenAI "OpenAI"), played a substantial role in the development and launch of artificial intelligence services such as [ChatGPT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT "ChatGPT"), [Codex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenAI_Codex "OpenAI Codex") and [DALL-E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DALL-E "DALL-E").[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-294)[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-295)[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-296) In December 2023, Prime Minister Edi Rama announced plans for collaboration between the Albanian government and ChatGPT, facilitated by discussions with Murati.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Taylor-297)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-298) Rama emphasised the intention to streamline the alignment of Albanian laws with the regulations of the European Union, aiming to reduce costs associated with translation and legal services.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Taylor-297)

## Demography

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Demography")]

Main article: [Demography of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Albania "Demographics of Albania")

See also: [Albanian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_people "Albanian people") and [Origin of the Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origin_of_the_Albanians "Origin of the Albanians")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Albania_demography.svg/220px-Albania_demography.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Albania_demography.svg)

Development of the [population of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Albania "Demographics of Albania") over the last sixty years

The demographic statistics of Albania, as revealed by the 2023 census conducted by the [Instituti i Statistikave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Statistics_\(Albania\) "Institute of Statistics (Albania)") (INSTAT), indicated a population of 2,402,113, with a notable decline from the 2,821,977 recorded in the 2011 census.[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024105-2)[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_Albanian_census20127-299) The decrease in inhabitants began after the disintegration of the communist regime in Albania and is associated with significant shifts within the political, economic, and social structure of Albania.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPopulation_Dynamics201425-300)[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024114-301) A principal factor in this transition incorporates a decline in fertility rates coupled with an increase in emigration, both contributing to persistent demographic changes and challenges.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPopulation_Dynamics201439-302) It is forecast that the population will continue shrinking for the next decade at least, depending on the actual rates and the level of migration.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-303) Currently, the population density of Albania is measured at 83.6 inhabitants per square kilometer with a varied distribution of inhabitants across different regions.[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024105-2)[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-304) The counties of [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_County "Tirana County") and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_County "Durrës County") showcase substantial concentrations of people, accounting for about 41% of the overall demographic of Albania, with 32% residing in Tirana and 9% in Durrës.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-305) Conversely, more peripheral and rural counties such as [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr_County "Gjirokastër County") and [Kukës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuk%C3%ABs_County "Kukës County") present significantly lower population densities, with each aiding 3% to the overall population.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-305)

Historically, the Albanian people have established several communities in many regions throughout Southern Europe. The [Albanian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_diaspora "Albanian diaspora") has been formed since the late Middle Ages, when they emigrated to escape either various socio-political difficulties or the Ottoman conquest of Albania.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-306) Following the fall of communism, large numbers of Albanians have migrated to countries such as Australia, Canada, Switzerland, the United Kingdom and the United States. Albanian [minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority_group "Minority group") are present in the neighbouring territories such as the west of North Macedonia, the southeast of Montenegro, Kosovo in its entirety and parts of southern Serbia. Altogether, the number of ethnic Albanians living abroad is estimated to be higher than the total population inside Albania. As much as a third of those born in the country's borders now live outside of it, making Albania one of the countries with the highest rate of outmigration relative to its population in the world.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-307)[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-308) In 2022 the birth rate was 20% lower than in 2021, largely due to emigration of people of childbearing age.[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-309)

### Urbanisation

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=36 "Edit section: Urbanisation")]

Main article: [List of cities and towns in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Albania "List of cities and towns in Albania")

Subsequent to the collapse of communism in 1991, Albania has undergone a remarkable transformation in its urban landscape, emerging as one of the fastest urbanising countries in Europe.[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201412-310)[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-311)[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-312) At the forefront of this transformation is the [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_County "Tirana County")-[Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_County "Durrës County") agglomeration, a densely populated urban corridor situated along the [western coast of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Coastal_Lowlands "Albanian Coastal Lowlands").[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIEACA_2-313) This corridor has become the primary locus of population growth and settlement development, attracting a significant influx of internal migrants from the country's peripheral areas.[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIEACA_2-313) Despite an overall decline of the country's total population, the proportion of the urban demographic has consistently progressed from 47% in 2001 to 65% in 2023.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024114-301)[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_Albanian_census201210-314)[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-315) This sustained increase, coupled with the concentration in the Tirana-Durrës region, has led to a spread of regional imbalances, with the peripheral areas, particularly [Dibër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dib%C3%ABr_County "Dibër County") and [Kukës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuk%C3%ABs_County "Kukës County"), experiencing severe depopulation.[\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201412_&_18-316)[\[315\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201415-317)[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-305)
