### Language

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=37 "Edit section: Language")]

Main article: [Languages of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Albania "Languages of Albania")

See also: [Albanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language")

|                                                                                                                                                   |                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png) | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(May 2023)* |

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Mesonjetorja_e_Pare_Shqipe_ne_Korce.JPG/220px-Mesonjetorja_e_Pare_Shqipe_ne_Korce.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mesonjetorja_e_Pare_Shqipe_ne_Korce.JPG)

Founded in 1887, the [Mësonjëtorja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%ABsonj%C3%ABtorja "Mësonjëtorja") was the first secular school to provide education in the [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") during the Ottoman Empire.

The [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language "Official language") of the country is [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") which is spoken by the vast majority of the country's population.[\[327\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-330) Its standard spoken and written form is revised and merged from the two main dialects, [Gheg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gheg "Gheg") and [Tosk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosk_Albanian "Tosk Albanian"), though it is notably based more on the Tosk dialect. The [Shkumbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shkumbin "Shkumbin") river is the rough dividing line between the two dialects. Among minority languages, [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language "Greek language") is the second most-spoken language in the country, with 0.5 to 3% of the population speaking it as first language, mainly in the country's south where its speakers are concentrated.[\[328\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-331)[\[329\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-332)[\[330\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SecondMostSpokenLanguage-333)[\[331\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-kathimerini-334) Other languages spoken by ethnic minorities in Albania include [Aromanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanian_language "Aromanian language"), [Serbian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_language "Serbian language"), [Macedonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonian_language "Macedonian language"), [Bosnian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_language "Bosnian language"), [Bulgarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarian_language "Bulgarian language"), [Gorani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gora_dialect "Gora dialect"), and [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_language "Romani language").[\[332\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-335) Macedonian is official in the Pustec Municipality in East Albania. According to the 2011 population census, 2,765,610 or 98.8% of the population declared [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") as their [mother tongue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_tongue "Mother tongue").[\[333\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Population_and_Housing_Census_2011-336) Because of large migration flows from Albania, over half of Albanians during their life learn a second language. The main foreign language known is English with 40.0%, followed by Italian with 27.8% and Greek with 22.9%. The English speakers were mostly young people, the knowledge of Italian is stable in every age group, while there is a decrease of the speakers of Greek in the youngest group.[\[334\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-337)

Among young people aged 25 or less, English, German and Turkish have seen rising interest after 2000. Italian and French have had a stable interest, while Greek has lost much of its previous interest. The trends are linked with cultural and economic factors.[\[335\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Shkurtaj2017-338)

Young people have shown a growing interest in the [German language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language "German language") in recent years.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*] Some of them go to Germany for studying or various experiences. Albania and Germany have agreements for cooperating in helping young people of the two countries know both cultures better.[\[336\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-339) Due to a sharp rise in economic relations with Turkey, interest in learning Turkish, in particular among young people, has been growing on a yearly basis.[\[337\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-340)

### Minorities

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=38 "Edit section: Minorities")]

Main article: [Minorities of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minorities_of_Albania "Minorities of Albania")

|                                                                                                                                                   |                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png) | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(September 2024)* |

Designated national and cultural minorities in Albania include Aromanian, Greek, Macedonian, Montenegrin, Serb, Roma, Egyptian, Bosnian and Bulgarian peoples, as well as Gorani and Jews as other minority groups.[\[338\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-341)[\[339\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-342) The 2023 census recorded the ethnic composition as [Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians "Albanians") 2,186,917 (91,04% of the total), [Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_in_Albania "Greeks in Albania") 23,485 (0,98%), [Macedonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonians_of_Albania "Macedonians of Albania") 2,281 (0,09%), [Montenegrins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montenegrins_of_Albania "Montenegrins of Albania") 511 (0,02%), [Aromanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanians_in_Albania "Aromanians in Albania") 2,459 (0,1%), [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_Albania "Romani people in Albania") 9,813 (0.4%), [Balkan Egyptians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkan_Egyptians "Balkan Egyptians") 12,375 (0,5%), [Bosnians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks_in_Albania "Bosniaks in Albania") 2,963 (0,12%), [Serbians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbs_and_Montenegrins_in_Albania "Serbs and Montenegrins in Albania") 584 (0,02%), [Bulgarians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarians_in_Albania "Bulgarians in Albania") 7,057 (0,29%), mixed ethnicities 770 (0.03%), other ethnicities 3,798 (0.15%), and 134,451 (5.60%) with unspecified ethnicity, out of a total population of 2,402,113.[\[340\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202475-343)

Contrary to official statistics that show an Albanian majority, the minority groups have frequently disputed the official numbers, asserting a higher percentage of the country's population. The Greek government claimed there were an estimation of 300,000 ethnic Greeks in Albania.[\[341\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-RFE/RL_Research_Report:_Weekly_Analyses_from_the_RFE/RL_Research_Institute-344)[\[342\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-BideleuxJeffries2006-345)[\[343\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Ramet1998-346)[\[344\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Jeffries2002-347)[\[345\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Publications2008-348) The [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook "CIA World Factbook") estimates the Greek minority to constitute 0.9%[\[346\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-349) of the population. The US State Department estimates that Greeks make up 1.17%, and other minorities 0.23%, of the population.[\[347\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-350) The latter questioned the validity of the 2011 census data about the Greek minority, as measurements had allegedly been affected by boycott.[\[348\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-351)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Albania_minorities.png/176px-Albania_minorities.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Albania_minorities.png)

Regions with a traditional presence of ethnic groups other than Albanian.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Albania_majority_ethnicity_2011_census.PNG/206px-Albania_majority_ethnicity_2011_census.PNG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Albania_majority_ethnicity_2011_census.PNG)

Distribution of ethnic groups within Albania, as of the 2011 census. Districts coloured grey are those where a majority of people did not declare an ethnicity (the question was optional). The census was criticised and boycotted by minorities in Albania.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/AlbaniaTraditionalCommunitiesByLanguageAndReligion.png/198px-AlbaniaTraditionalCommunitiesByLanguageAndReligion.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:AlbaniaTraditionalCommunitiesByLanguageAndReligion.png)

Traditional locations of linguistic and religious communities in Albania.

### Religion

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=39 "Edit section: Religion")]

Main article: [Religion in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Albania "Religion in Albania")

See also: [Albanian paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_paganism "Albanian paganism")

[Religion in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Albania "Religion in Albania") as of the 2023 census conducted by the Instituti i Statistikës (INSTAT)[\[1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202476-1)

  [Sunni Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam "Sunni Islam") (45.86%)

  [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism "Roman Catholicism") (8.38%)

  [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy "Eastern Orthodoxy") (7.22%)

  Evangelical Christians (0.4%)

  [Bektashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi "Bektashi") (4.81%)

  Non-affiliated believers (13.82%)

  [Atheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism "Atheism") (3.55%)

  Undeclared (15.76%)

  Other (0.15%)

Albania is a [secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state "Secular state") and religiously diverse country with no [official religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_religion "Official religion"). [Freedom of religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion "Freedom of religion"), [belief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_belief "Freedom of belief") and [conscience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_conscience "Freedom of conscience") are guaranteed under the country's [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Albania "Constitution of Albania").[\[349\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-352) As of the 2023 Census, there were 1,101,718 (45.86%) [Sunni Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam "Sunni Islam"), 201,530 (8.38%) [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism "Roman Catholicism"), 173,645 (7.22%) [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy "Eastern Orthodoxy"), 115,644 (4.81%) [Bektashi Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi_Order "Bektashi Order"), 9,658 (0.4%) [Evangelicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism "Evangelicalism"), 3,670 (0.15%) of other religions, 332,155 (13.82%) believers without denomination, 85,311 (3.55%) Atheists and 378,782 (15.76%) did not provide an answer.[\[1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202476-1) Albania is nevertheless ranked among the least religious countries in the world.[\[350\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-353) Religion constitute an important role in the lives of only 39% of the country's population.[\[351\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-354) In another report, 56% considered themselves religious, 30% considered themselves non-religious, while 9% defined themselves as convinced atheists. 80% believed in God.[\[352\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-355)

The [Muslim Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Albania "Islam in Albania") are spread throughout the country. [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodoxy_in_Albania "Orthodoxy in Albania") and [Bektashis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi "Bektashi") are mostly found in the south, whereas [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism_in_Albania "Catholicism in Albania") mainly live in the north.[\[353\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-International_Religious_Freedom_Report_2007-356) In 2008, there were 694 [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic "Catholic") churches and 425 [orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church "Eastern Orthodox Church") churches, 568 mosques and 70 [bektashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi_Order "Bektashi Order") [takyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takya "Takya") in the country.[\[354\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-357)[\[355\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-358)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Marche_Charlie_Hebdo_Paris_02.jpg/220px-Marche_Charlie_Hebdo_Paris_02.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Marche_Charlie_Hebdo_Paris_02.jpg)

Representatives of the Sunni, Orthodox, Bektashi and Catholic Albanian communities in [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris "Paris")

During [modern times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_history "Modern history"), the Albanian republican, monarchic and later communist regimes followed a systematic policy of separating religion from official functions and cultural life. The country has never had an [official religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_religion "Official religion") either as a republic or as a kingdom. In the 20th century, the clergy of all faiths was weakened under the monarchy and ultimately eradicated during the 1950s and 1960s, under the state policy of obliterating all organised religion from the territories of Albania. The [communist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism_in_Albania "Communism in Albania") persecuted and suppressed religious observance and institutions and entirely [banned religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-religious "Anti-religious"). The country was then officially declared to be the world's first [atheist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist_state "Atheist state"). Religious freedom has returned, however, since the [end of communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_communism_in_Albania "Fall of communism in Albania").

Islam survived communist era persecution and reemerged in the modern era as a practised religion in Albania.[\[356\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Merdjanova673940-359) Some smaller Christian sects in Albania include [Evangelicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism "Evangelicalism") and several [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant "Protestant") communities including [Seventh-day Adventist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church "Seventh-day Adventist Church"), [Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints "The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints") and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses "Jehovah's Witnesses").[\[357\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-360)[\[358\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-361)[\[359\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-362)[\[360\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-363) The first recorded Protestant of Albania was Said Toptani, who travelled around Europe and returned to [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") in 1853, where he preached [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism "Protestantism"). The first [evangelical Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Protestants "Evangelical Protestants") appeared in the 19th century and the Evangelical Alliance was founded in 1892. Nowadays, it has 160 member congregations from different Protestant denominations. Following mass emigration to Israel after the fall of communism, there are only 200 Albanian [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_in_Albania "Judaism in Albania") left in the country.[\[361\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-364)[\[362\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-365)

## Culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=40 "Edit section: Culture")]

Main article: [Culture of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Albania "Culture of Albania")

### Symbols

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=41 "Edit section: Symbols")]

Main article: [National symbols of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Albania "National symbols of Albania")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Casque_Skanderbeg_Vienne.jpg/220px-Casque_Skanderbeg_Vienne.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Casque_Skanderbeg_Vienne.jpg)

The [helmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_of_Skanderbeg "Arms of Skanderbeg") of [Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg "Skanderbeg") stands as an emblem of Albanian independence

The red and black [national colours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_colours "National colours") are displayed on the [flag of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Albania "Flag of Albania") that is adorned with a [double-headed eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-headed_eagle "Double-headed eagle") as the foremost recognised [national symbol of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Albania "National symbols of Albania").[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flamuri-366)[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flag_Elsie-367)[\[365\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Minahan-368) With the black eagle portraying bravery and strength, the red field symbolises the fortitude and sacrifices of the Albanian people.[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flamuri-366) The eagle is linked with the legacy of the national hero [Gjergj Kastrioti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg "Skanderbeg"), renowned as Skanderbeg, who led a prosperous [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg%27s_rebellion "Skanderbeg's rebellion") against Ottoman rule.[\[366\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-369)[\[367\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-370)[\[365\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Minahan-368) Rooted in the Middle Ages, it emerged as a [heraldic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_heraldry "Albanian heraldry") [symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armorial_of_Albania "Armorial of Albania") in the [Principality of Arbër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Arbanon "Principality of Arbanon") and among notable Albanian dynasties such as the [Dukagjini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukagjini_family "Dukagjini family"), [Kastrioti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kastrioti_family "Kastrioti family"), [Muzaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzaka_family "Muzaka family") and [Thopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thopia_family "Thopia family") clans.[\[368\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-371) Amid the [Albanian Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_National_Awakening "Albanian National Awakening"), marking the resurgence of Albanian national identity and aspirations for independence, the Albanian eagle regained its prominence.[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flag_Elsie-367) Its importance reached its culmination with [Albania's declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Declaration_of_Independence "Albanian Declaration of Independence") in 1912, when [Ismail Qemali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Qemali "Ismail Qemali") raised it as the national flag in [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB "Vlorë").[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flag_Elsie-367)

The [coat of arms of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Albania "Coat of arms of Albania") is an adaptation of the flag of Albania and the seal of Skanderbeg.[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flamuri-366) The coat comprises the black double-headed eagle positioned at the centre of a red field.[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flamuri-366) Above the eagle, it carries the [helmet of Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_of_Skanderbeg "Arms of Skanderbeg") that is crowned with the head of a golden horned goat.[\[365\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Minahan-368) [Ti Shqipëri, më jep nder, më jep emrin Shqipëtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ti_Shqip%C3%ABri,_m%C3%AB_jep_nder,_m%C3%AB_jep_emrin_Shqip%C3%ABtar "Ti Shqipëri, më jep nder, më jep emrin Shqipëtar"), which translates to "You Albania, give me honour, give me the name Albanian", is the [national motto of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_mottos "List of national mottos").[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flag_Elsie-367)[\[365\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Minahan-368) It finds its foundation in the legacy of [national poet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_poet "National poet") [Naim Frashëri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naim_Frash%C3%ABri "Naim Frashëri"), who held a renowned role during the Albanian Renaissance.[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flag_Elsie-367) The [national anthem of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_anthem "National anthem"), "[Himni i Flamurit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himni_i_Flamurit "Himni i Flamurit")", was composed by [Asdreni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksand%C3%ABr_Stavre_Drenova "Aleksandër Stavre Drenova") and adopted as such following Albania's independence in 1912.[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Flamuri-366)

### Clothing

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=42 "Edit section: Clothing")]

Main article: [Albanian traditional clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_national_clothing "Albanian national clothing")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Les_Danseurs_Albanais_by_Alexandre-Gabriel_Decamps_%28c._1835%29.jpg/220px-Les_Danseurs_Albanais_by_Alexandre-Gabriel_Decamps_%28c._1835%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Les_Danseurs_Albanais_by_Alexandre-Gabriel_Decamps_\(c._1835\).jpg)

The [Fustanella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fustanella "Fustanella") is designated as the [national costume of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_costume_of_Albania "National costume of Albania") with profound cultural significance within Albanian culture

[Albanian traditional clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_national_clothing "Albanian national clothing"), with its contrasting variations for the northern [Gheg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghegs "Ghegs") and southern [Tosk Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosks "Tosks"), is a testament to Albania's history, cultural diversity and ethnic identity.[\[369\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gjergji-372) Gheg men wear a light-colored shirt paired with wide loose-fitting trousers referred to as the Tirq. These trousers are securely fastened by a wide woven belt, the Brez. An integral component of their costume is the [Xhamadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhamadan "Xhamadan"), a woolen red velvet vest adorned with folkloric motifs and gold patterns.[\[370\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-373) They also wear a distinctive dome-shaped hat known as the [Qeleshe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qeleshe "Qeleshe"), crafted from wool, with its origins tracing back to the [Illyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrians "Illyrians").[\[371\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-374)[\[372\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-375) Conversely, Tosk men opt for the [Fustanella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fustanella "Fustanella"), a knee-length garment designated as the national costume, typically white with pleats, accompanied by a white shirt. They also wear a beige or dark blue Xhamadan reminiscent of the Gheg attire. To complete their attire, men wear knee-high socks referred to as [Çorape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%87orape "Çorape") and leather shoes known as [Opinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinga "Opinga").

The attire of Gheg and Tosk women is renowned for its rich palette of colors and intricate filigree detailing, displayed across various components including ear ornaments, finger rings, necklaces, belt buckles and buttons. Key components include a long, light-toned shirt paired with an apron, often accompanied by a woolen or felt mantle referred to as the Xhoka. Additional upper garments, such as the Dollama or Mintan, may be layered, along with a headscarf known as Kapica or Shall. A distinctive variation incorporates a pair of aprons, including the Pështjellak at the rear and the Paranik at the front. Integral to the traditional attire of Gheg women is the ancient [Xhubleta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhubleta "Xhubleta").[\[369\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gjergji-372) Dedicated efforts have been undertaken to preserve and promote the dress, acknowledging its significance as an emblem of Albanian heritage.[\[373\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Xhubleta-376) As a testament to its importance, the Xhubleta has been inscribed on the [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [Intangible Cultural Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists "UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage Lists").[\[373\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Xhubleta-376) The distinctive bell-shaped garment is fashioned in black and enriched with embroidered ethnic Albanian motifs, showcasing the remarkable craftsmanship of northern Albania.[\[369\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gjergji-372) Its crafting process involves multiple intricate stages, encompassing the preparation of shajak fabric and the precise cutting techniques.[\[373\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Xhubleta-376)

### Art and architecture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=43 "Edit section: Art and architecture")]

Main articles: [Albanian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_art "Albanian art") and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_architecture "Albanian architecture")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Amphitheatre_of_Butrint_2009.jpg/220px-Amphitheatre_of_Butrint_2009.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Amphitheatre_of_Butrint_2009.jpg)

[Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint "Butrint") has been included in the [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") list of [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site "World Heritage Site") since 1992

The [artistic history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_art "Albanian art") of Albania has been particularly influenced by a multitude of ancient and medieval [people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilisation "Civilisation"), traditions and religions. It covers a broad spectrum with mediums and disciplines that include painting, [pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pottery "Pottery"), sculpture, [ceramics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceramic "Ceramic") and architecture all of them exemplifying a great variety in style and shape, in different regions and period.

The rise of the [Byzantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire "Byzantine Empire") and [Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Empire "Ottoman Empire") in the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages "Middle Ages") was accompanied by a corresponding growth in [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_art "Christian art") and [Islamic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_art "Islamic art") in the lands of Albania which are apparent in examples of architecture and mosaics throughout the country.[\[374\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-www.albanianart.net_index_al-377) Centuries later, the [Albanian Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Renaissance "Albanian Renaissance") proved crucial to the emancipation of the modern Albanian culture and saw unprecedented developments in all fields of literature and art whereas artists sought to return to the ideals of [Impressionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism "Impressionism") and [Romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism_\(art\) "Romanticism (art)").[\[375\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-378) However, [Onufri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onufri "Onufri"), [Kolë Idromeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kol%C3%AB_Idromeno "Kolë Idromeno"), [David Selenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Selenica "David Selenica"), [Kostandin Shpataraku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostandin_Shpataraku "Kostandin Shpataraku") and the [Zografi Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zografi_Brothers "Zografi Brothers") are the most eminent representatives of Albanian art.

The [architecture of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Albania "Architecture of Albania") reflects the legacy of various civilisations tracing back to the [classical antiquity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_antiquity "Classical antiquity"). Major cities in Albania have evolved from within the castle to include dwellings, religious and commercial structures, with constant redesigning of town squares and evolution of building techniques. Nowadays, the cities and towns reflect a whole spectrum of various [architectural styles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_style "Architectural style"). In the 20th century, many historical as well as [sacred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_architecture "Sacred architecture") buildings bearing the ancient influence were demolished during the [communist era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism_in_Albania "Communism in Albania").[\[376\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-379)

[Ancient architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_architecture "Ancient architecture") is found throughout Albania and most visible in [Byllis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byllis "Byllis"), [Amantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amantia "Amantia"), [Phoenice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenice "Phoenice"), [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\) "Apollonia (Illyria)"), [Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint "Butrint"), [Antigonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antigonia_\(Chaonia\) "Antigonia (Chaonia)"), [Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozafa_Castle "Rozafa Castle") and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_Amphitheatre "Durrës Amphitheatre"). Considering the long period of rule of the [Byzantine Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire "Byzantine Empire"), they introduced castles, citadels, churches and monasteries with spectacular wealth of visible [murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural "Mural") and [frescos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresco "Fresco"). Perhaps the best known examples can be found in the southern Albanian cities and surroundings of [Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB "Korçë"), [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat "Berat"), [Voskopojë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voskopoj%C3%AB "Voskopojë") and [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr "Gjirokastër"). Involving the introduction of [Ottoman architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_architecture "Ottoman architecture") there was a development of mosques and other Islamic buildings, particularly seen in Berat and Gjirokastër.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Bliss_by_Helidon_Xhixza.jpg/220px-Bliss_by_Helidon_Xhixza.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bliss_by_Helidon_Xhixza.jpg)

*Bliss*, stainless steel sculpture by contemporary artist [Helidon Xhixha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helidon_Xhixha "Helidon Xhixha").

A productive period of [Historicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historicism_\(art\) "Historicism (art)"), [Art Nouveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Nouveau "Art Nouveau") and [Neoclassicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassicism "Neoclassicism") merged into the 19th century, best exemplified in [Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB "Korçë"). The 20th century brought new architectural styles such as the modern [Italian style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italianate_architecture "Italianate architecture"), which is present in [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana") such as the [Skanderbeg Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Square "Skanderbeg Square") and Ministries. It is also present in Shkodër, [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB "Vlorë"), [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB "Sarandë") and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs "Durrës"). Moreover, other towns received their present-day Albania-unique appearance through various cultural or economic influences.

[Socialist classicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_classicism "Socialist classicism") arrived during the [communist era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoples_Republic_of_Albania "Peoples Republic of Albania") in Albania after the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War "Second World War"). At this period many socialist-styled complexes, wide roads and factories were constructed, while town squares were redesigned and numerous of historic and important buildings demolished. Notable examples of that style include the [Mother Teresa Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Teresa_Square_\(Tirana\) "Mother Teresa Square (Tirana)"), [Pyramid of Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_of_Tirana "Pyramid of Tirana"), [Palace of Congresses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Congresses "Palace of Congresses") and so on.

Three Albanian [archaeological](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeology "Archaeology") sites are included in the list of [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site "UNESCO World Heritage Site"). These include the ancient remains of Butrint, the medieval [Historic Centres of Berat and Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Centres_of_Berat_and_Gjirokast%C3%ABr "Historic Centres of Berat and Gjirokastër"), and [Natural and Cultural Heritage of the Ohrid region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohrid_Lake "Ohrid Lake") site shared with [North Macedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Macedonia "North Macedonia") since 2019.[\[377\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-380)[\[378\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-381) Furthermore, the royal [Illyrian tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Tombs_of_Selca_e_Poshtme "Royal Tombs of Selca e Poshtme"), the remains of [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\) "Apollonia (Illyria)"), the ancient [Amphitheatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_Amphitheatre "Durrës Amphitheatre") of Durrës and the [Fortress of Bashtovë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortress_of_Bashtov%C3%AB "Fortress of Bashtovë") has been included on the tentative list of Albania.

### Cuisine

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=44 "Edit section: Cuisine")]

Main article: [Albanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_cuisine "Albanian cuisine")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Buk%C3%AB_kollomoqe%2C_buk%C3%AB_misri.JPG/220px-Buk%C3%AB_kollomoqe%2C_buk%C3%AB_misri.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Buk%C3%AB_kollomoqe,_buk%C3%AB_misri.JPG)

Bukë misri ([cornbread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornbread "Cornbread")) is a staple on the Albanian table

Throughout the centuries, [Albanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_cuisine "Albanian cuisine") has been widely influenced by [Albanian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_culture "Albanian culture"), [geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Albania "Geography of Albania") and [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Albania "History of Albania"), and as such, different parts of the country enjoy specific [regional cuisines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_cuisine "Regional cuisine"). Cooking traditions especially vary between the north and the south, owing to differing [topography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topography "Topography") and [climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate "Climate") that essentially contribute to the excellent growth conditions for a wide array of herbs, fruits, and vegetables.[\[379\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-382)

Albanians produce and use many varieties of fruits such as [lemons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemon_\(fruit\) "Lemon (fruit)"), oranges, [figs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figs_\(fruit\) "Figs (fruit)"), and most notably, [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive_\(fruit\) "Olive (fruit)"), which are perhaps the most important element of Albanian cooking. Spices and other herbs such as [basil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basil_\(herb\) "Basil (herb)"), [lavender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavender "Lavender"), [mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spearmint "Spearmint"), [oregano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregano "Oregano"), [rosemary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemary "Rosemary"), and [thyme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thyme "Thyme") are widely used, as are vegetables such as garlic, onions, [peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_pepper "Bell pepper"), potatoes, tomatoes, as well as [legumes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume "Legume") of all types.

With a coastline along the [Adriatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Adriatic_Sea_Coast "Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast") and [Ionian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Ionian_Sea_Coast "Albanian Ionian Sea Coast") in the [Mediterranean Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Sea "Mediterranean Sea"), fish, [crustaceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean "Crustacean"), and seafood are a popular and an integral part of the Albanian diet. Otherwise, [lamb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_\(food\) "Lamb (food)") is the traditional meat for different holidays and [religious festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_festival "Religious festival") for both [Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Albania "Christianity in Albania") and [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Albania "Islam in Albania"), although poultry, beef, and pork are also in plentiful supply.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Albanian_cuisine_-_Pite_dhe_Speca.jpg/220px-Albanian_cuisine_-_Pite_dhe_Speca.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Albanian_cuisine_-_Pite_dhe_Speca.jpg)

Speca të fërguar (roasted [peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capsicum "Capsicum")) served with [pite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pite_\(food\) "Pite (food)"), a traditional and prominent layered Albanian pie

[Tavë kosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tav%C3%AB_kosi "Tavë kosi") ("[soured milk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soured_milk "Soured milk") [casserole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casserole "Casserole")") is the [national dish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_dish "National dish") of Albania, consisting of lamb and rice baked under a thick, tart veil of [yoghurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoghurt "Yoghurt"). [Fërgesë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%ABrges%C3%AB "Fërgesë") is another national dish, made up of peppers, tomatoes, and [cottage cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cottage_cheese "Cottage cheese"). Pite is also popular, a baked pastry with a filling of a mixture of [spinach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinach "Spinach") and gjizë (curd) or mish ([ground meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_meat "Ground meat")).

Petulla, a traditional fried dough, is also a popular speciality, and is served with [powdered sugar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powdered_sugar "Powdered sugar") or [feta cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feta_cheese "Feta cheese") and different sorts of [fruit jams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruit_preserves "Fruit preserves"). [Flia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flia "Flia") consists of multiple [crêpe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cr%C3%AApe "Crêpe")-like layers brushed with cream and served with sour cream. [Krofne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krofne "Krofne"), similar to [Berliner doughnuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berliner_\(doughnut\) "Berliner (doughnut)"), are filled with jam, or chocolate and often eaten during winter.

Coffee is an integral part of the Albanian lifestyle. The country has more coffee houses per capita than any other country in the world.[\[380\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-383) Tea is also enjoyed both at home or outside at cafés, bars, or restaurants. Çaj Mali ([Sideritis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sideritis "Sideritis") tea) is enormously beloved, and a part of the daily routine for most Albanians. It is cultivated across Southern Albania and noted for its medicinal properties. [Black tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_tea "Black tea") is also popular.

[Albanian wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_wine "Albanian wine") is also common throughout the country, and has been cultivated for thousands of years. Albania has a long and ancient history of wine production, and belongs to the [Old World of wine producing countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_World_wine "Old World wine").[\[381\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-384)[\[382\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-385)

### Music

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=45 "Edit section: Music")]

Main article: [Music of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Albania "Music of Albania")

See also: [Albanian war dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_war_dances "Albanian war dances")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/A_traditional_male_folk_group_from_Skrapar.JPG/220px-A_traditional_male_folk_group_from_Skrapar.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A_traditional_male_folk_group_from_Skrapar.JPG)

[Albanian iso-polyphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_iso-polyphony "Albanian iso-polyphony") is designated as an [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [Masterpiece of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity "Masterpieces of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity").[\[383\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-386)

Albanian folk music is a prominent part of the national identity, and continues to play a major part in overall [Albanian music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Albania "Music of Albania"). Folk music can be divided into two stylistic groups, mainly the northern [Gheg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gheg_Albanian "Gheg Albanian") varieties, and southern [Lab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lab%C3%ABria "Labëria") and [Tosk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosk_Albanian "Tosk Albanian") varieties. Northern and southern traditions are contrasted by a rugged tone from the north, and the more relaxed southern form of music.

Many songs concern events from [Albanian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Albania "History of Albania") and [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Albania "Culture of Albania"), including traditional themes of honour, hospitality, treachery, and revenge. The first compilation of Albanian folk music was made by two [Himariot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himara "Himara") musicians, [Neço Muka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ne%C3%A7o_Muko "Neço Muko") and Koço Çakali, in Paris, during their work with Albanian soprano [Tefta Tashko-Koço](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tefta_Tashko-Ko%C3%A7o "Tefta Tashko-Koço"). Several [gramophone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phonograph_record "Phonograph record") compilations were recorded at the time by the three artists, which eventually led to the recognition of [Albanian iso-polyphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_iso-polyphony "Albanian iso-polyphony") as a [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists "UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage Lists").[\[384\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-387)

[Festivali i Këngës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festivali_i_K%C3%ABng%C3%ABs "Festivali i Këngës") is a traditional Albanian song contest organised by the national broadcaster [Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar "Radio Televizioni Shqiptar") (RTSH). The festival is celebrated annually since its inauguration in 1962 and has launched the careers of some of Albania's most successful singers including [Vaçe Zela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Va%C3%A7e_Zela "Vaçe Zela") and [Parashqevi Simaku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parashqevi_Simaku "Parashqevi Simaku").[\[385\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-388) It is significantly a music competition among Albanian performers presenting unreleased songs in premiere, composed by Albanian authors and voted by juries or by public.

Contemporary artists [Rita Ora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Ora "Rita Ora"), [Bebe Rexha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebe_Rexha "Bebe Rexha"), [Era Istrefi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Era_Istrefi "Era Istrefi"), [Dua Lipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dua_Lipa "Dua Lipa"), [Ava Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ava_Max "Ava Max"), [Bleona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bleona "Bleona"), [Elvana Gjata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elvana_Gjata "Elvana Gjata"), [Ermonela Jaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermonela_Jaho "Ermonela Jaho"), and [Inva Mula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inva_Mula "Inva Mula") have achieved international recognition for their music,[\[386\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-389) while soprano [Ermonela Jaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermonela_Jaho "Ermonela Jaho") has been described by some as the "world's most acclaimed soprano".[\[387\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-390) Albanian opera singer [Saimir Pirgu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saimir_Pirgu "Saimir Pirgu") was nominated for the 2017 [Grammy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award "Grammy Award").[\[388\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-391)

### Literature

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=46 "Edit section: Literature")]

Main article: [Albanian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_literature "Albanian literature")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Meshari.jpg/220px-Meshari.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Meshari.jpg)

An excerpt from the [Meshari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meshari "Meshari") *(The Missal)* written by [Gjon Buzuku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjon_Buzuku "Gjon Buzuku") (1555)

The cultural renaissance was first of all expressed through the development of the [Albanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language") in the area of church texts and publications. The Protestant reforms invigorated hopes for the development of the local language and literary tradition, when cleric [Gjon Buzuku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjon_Buzuku "Gjon Buzuku") translated the [Catholic liturgy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_liturgy "Catholic liturgy") into Albanian, trying to do for Albanian what [Martin Luther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther "Martin Luther") had done for German. [Meshari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meshari "Meshari") (*The Missal*) written by [Gjon Buzuku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjon_Buzuku "Gjon Buzuku") was published in 1555 and is considered one of the first literary work of written Albanian during the Middle Ages. The refined level of the language and the stabilised orthography must be the result of an earlier tradition of written Albanian, a tradition that is not well understood. However, there is some fragmented evidence, pre-dating Buzuku, which indicates that Albanian was written from at least the 14th century.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Parashqevi_Qiriazi.jpg/170px-Parashqevi_Qiriazi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Parashqevi_Qiriazi.jpg)

[Parashqevi Qiriazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parashqevi_Qiriazi "Parashqevi Qiriazi") – teacher and feminist (1880–1970)

Albanian writings from these centuries must not have been religious texts only, but historical chronicles too. They are mentioned by the humanist [Marin Barleti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_Barleti "Marin Barleti"), who in his book [Siege of Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Shkodra_\(1478\)#The_Book "Siege of Shkodra (1478)") (*Rrethimi i Shkodrës*) from 1504, confirms that he leafed through such chronicles written in the language of the people (*in vernacula lingua*) as well as his famous biography of [Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg "Skanderbeg") [Historia de vita et gestis Scanderbegi Epirotarum principis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_Barleti "Marin Barleti") (*History of Skanderbeg*) from 1508. The *History of Skanderbeg* is still the foundation of Skanderbeg studies and is considered an Albanian cultural treasure, vital to the formation of Albanian national self-consciousness.

During the 16th and the 17th centuries, the [catechism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism "Catechism") (*E mbësuame krishterë*) (Christian Teachings) from 1592 written by [Lekë Matrënga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lek%C3%AB_Matr%C3%ABnga "Lekë Matrënga"), (*Doktrina e krishterë*) (The Christian Doctrine) from 1618 and (*Rituale romanum*) 1621 by [Pjetër Budi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pjet%C3%ABr_Budi "Pjetër Budi"), the first writer of original Albanian [prose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prose "Prose") and poetry, an [apology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remorse "Remorse") for George Castriot (1636) by [Frang Bardhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frang_Bardhi "Frang Bardhi"), who also published a dictionary and [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore "Folklore") creations, the theological-philosophical treaty *[Cuneus Prophetarum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuneus_Prophetarum "Cuneus Prophetarum")* (The Band of Prophets) (1685) by [Pjetër Bogdani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pjet%C3%ABr_Bogdani "Pjetër Bogdani"), the most universal personality of Albanian [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages "Middle Ages"), were published in [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language"). The most famous Albanian writer in the 20th and 21st century is probably [Ismail Kadare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Kadare "Ismail Kadare").

### Sports

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=47 "Edit section: Sports")]

See also: [Albania at the Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_at_the_Olympics "Albania at the Olympics") and [Albania at the Mediterranean Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_at_the_Mediterranean_Games "Albania at the Mediterranean Games")

Albania participated at the [Olympic Games in 1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics "1972 Summer Olympics") for the first time. The country made their Winter Olympic Games debut in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winter_Olympics_2006 "Winter Olympics 2006"). Albania missed the next four games, two of them due to the 1980 and 1984 boycotts, but returned for the 1992 games in [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Olympic_Winter_Games "1992 Olympic Winter Games"). Since then, Albania have participated in all games. Albania normally competes in events that include swimming, athletics, weightlifting, shooting and wrestling. The country have been represented by the [National Olympic Committee of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Olympic_Committee_of_Albania "National Olympic Committee of Albania") since 1972. The nation has participated at the [Mediterranean Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Games "Mediterranean Games") since the games of [1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Mediterranean_Games "1987 Mediterranean Games") in Syria. The Albanian athletes have won a total of 43 (8 gold, 17 silver and 18 bronze) medals from [1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Mediterranean_Games "1987 Mediterranean Games") to [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Mediterranean_Games "2013 Mediterranean Games").

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Muzeu_Arkeologjike_Tirane.jpg/220px-Muzeu_Arkeologjike_Tirane.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Muzeu_Arkeologjike_Tirane.jpg)

[Arena Kombëtare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arena_Komb%C3%ABtare "Arena Kombëtare") in central Tirana

Popular sports in Albania include [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Albania "Football in Albania"), weightlifting, [basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_national_basketball_team "Albania national basketball team"), volleyball, tennis, swimming, [rugby union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_union "Rugby union") and gymnastics. Football is by far the most popular sport in Albania. It is governed by the [Football Association of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Association_of_Albania "Football Association of Albania") ([Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language "Albanian language"): *Federata Shqiptare e Futbollit*, F.SH.F.), which has membership in [FIFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA "FIFA") and [UEFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA "UEFA").

The [Albania national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_national_football_team "Albania national football team"), ranking 51st in the [World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Rankings "FIFA World Rankings") in 2017 (highest 22nd on 22 August 2015) have won the [1946 Balkan Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Balkan_Cup "1946 Balkan Cup") and the [Malta Rothmans International Tournament 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rothmans_International_Tournament_2000 "Rothmans International Tournament 2000"), but had never participated in any major [UEFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA "UEFA") or [FIFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA "FIFA") tournament, until [UEFA Euro 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2016 "UEFA Euro 2016"), Albania's first ever appearance at the continental tournament and at a major men's football tournament. Albania scored their first ever goal in a major tournament and secured their first ever win in European Championship when they beat [Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania_national_football_team "Romania national football team") by 1–0 in a [UEFA Euro 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2016 "UEFA Euro 2016") match on 19 June 2016.[\[389\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-392)[\[390\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-393) The most successful football clubs are [Skënderbeu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF_Sk%C3%ABnderbeu_Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB "KF Skënderbeu Korçë"), [KF Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF_Tirana "KF Tirana"), [Dinamo Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FK_Dinamo_Tirana "FK Dinamo Tirana"), [Partizani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FK_Partizani_Tirana "FK Partizani Tirana") and [Vllaznia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KF_Vllaznia_Shkod%C3%ABr "KF Vllaznia Shkodër").

Weightlifting is one of the most successful individual sport for the Albanians, with the national team winning medals at the [European Weightlifting Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Weightlifting_Championships "European Weightlifting Championships") and the rest international competitions. Albanian weightlifters have won a total of 16 medals at the [European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Weightlifting_Championships "European Weightlifting Championships") with 1 of them being gold, 7 silver and 8 bronze. In the [World Weightlifting Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Weightlifting_Championships "World Weightlifting Championships"), the Albanian weightlifting team has won in [1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_World_Weightlifting_Championships "1972 World Weightlifting Championships") a gold in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_World_Weightlifting_Championships "2002 World Weightlifting Championships") a silver and in [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_World_Weightlifting_Championships "2011 World Weightlifting Championships") a bronze medal.

## Human rights

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Albania\&action=edit\&section=48 "Edit section: Human rights")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Tirana_Gay%28P%29Ride_2016.jpg/220px-Tirana_Gay%28P%29Ride_2016.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tirana_Gay\(P\)Ride_2016.jpg)

2016 LGBT+ Pride in [Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana "Tirana").

Nearly 60% of women in rural areas suffer physical or psychological violence and nearly 8% are victims of sexual violence. Protection orders are often violated. In 2014 the Albanian Helsinki Committee (AHC) reported that the number of female murder victims is still high.[\[391\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-civilRightsDefenders-394) The Commissioner for Protection from Discrimination has raised concerns regarding the family registration law that discriminates against women. As a result, heads of households, who are overwhelmingly men, have the right to change family residency without their partners' permission.[\[391\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-civilRightsDefenders-394)

In 2015, the association [ILGA-Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILGA-Europe "ILGA-Europe") ranked Albania 19th in terms of LGBT rights out of 49 observed European countries.[\[392\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-395) Meanwhile, on the latest report in 2022, lack of progress caused Albania to be ranked the 28th country in Europe, among 49 countries observed.[\[393\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-396)

[ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\) "ISBN (identifier)") [9789928234049](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/9789928234049 "Special:BookSources/9789928234049"). "Sic u permend me lart, per shkak te shkaqeve kulturore dhe ekonomike, trendet e mesimit te gjuheve nga te rinjte (grupmosha deri ne 25 vjec) ndryshojne. Keto trende jane percaktues i nje sere fenomeneve shoqerore, sic do te shohim me tej. Keshtu nga viti 2000 e ketej, gjuha angleze, gjermane dhe ajo turke kane pasur nje rritje te interest. Gjuha italiane, por edhe ajo franceze kane pasur nje stabilitet, pra as rritje dhe as ulje te interesit te pergjithshem nga ana e grupmoshes te siperpermendur. Vihet re se gjuha greke ka pesuar nje renie te forte te interesit. Ne fakt, shumica e interesit ka rene per kete gjuhe. Arsyet per kete gjuhe specifike do ti trajtojme me tej ne kapitulin e trete."
